## 9. Architecture and code review, 2026-09-12

The author's request for a comprehensive review adds a findings record to
this root before opening focused research children. The review covers the
engine, compiler, HTTP/CLI host, both client tiers and stores, React arms,
reference apps, simulator, fixtures, witnesses and release tooling at
Snapback 4 tree `cf0a13dca32b3e1721d1d947a3f82c262a0807f0`
(repository commit `d6c803878a5432cce5f045777786a049972d3d70`, version
0.0.16). It does not change the bakeoff decisions or accept new designs.

The prescribed suite passes, but additional probes reproduce
schema-admission and projection failures, lost or regressed replica state,
incorrect predictions, stale observations, compiler semantic errors and
request-worker starvation. Passing the existing suite does not establish
the missing properties. The author's updated scope reserves security holes
for another model; this record covers architecture, correctness,
performance and ergonomics and makes no security-review claim.

Four questions need design work beyond an isolated patch:

- [LLP 3000.000](./3000.000-atomic-schema-and-release-activation.research.md):
  the atomic unit and owner of schema/release activation.
- [LLP 3000.001](./3000.001-durable-replica-and-write-lifecycle.research.md):
  confirmed replica data, speculative writes and durable outcomes.
- [LLP 3000.002](./3000.002-end-to-end-resource-bounds.research.md):
  physical resource bounds across requests and replication.
- [LLP 3000.003](./3000.003-one-semantics-and-credible-verification.research.md):
  one semantic contract across Rust, TypeScript and verification hosts.

The [detailed review and 37-ticket index](./reviews/3000-architecture-code-review-20260912.md)
record evidence, alternatives and acceptance examples. All four children are
Draft Research. They remain review findings and proposals; the core owner
retains implementation custody under §1 decision 4.

The author subsequently requested implementation of the 37 concrete tickets.
All 37 are now closed; the [correction qualification record](./reviews/3000-review-corrections-20260913.md)
contains the executed checks, independent reviews and remaining research scope.
Their resolution records and the children's `Implementation follow-up`
sections describe the delivered boundaries: transactional semantic activation,
confirmed-base replay and atomic snapshot ownership, bounded admission/output,
and compiler/client parity corrections. Integration preserves the concurrently
landed native Expo module and ordered-merge performance work. The four children
remain Draft Research for their explicitly outstanding choices; the ticket
fixes do not claim that every architecture question has been settled.

The separate [security review](./reviews/3000-security-review-20260913.md)
opened twelve findings across the HTTP, identity, storage, synchronization and
asset boundaries. Its
[remediation record](./reviews/3000-security-remediation-20260913.md) closes
those findings and three overlapping comprehensive-review findings. The four
security decisions in §7 state the lasting contract; individual tickets retain
the counterexamples, acceptance evidence and limits.

## How to orient

Read this document, then LLP 2000.000 for the argument and its evidence,
then `snapback4/AGENTS.md` once it exists, then the journal. For the
sibling's position read LLP 2000; for the incumbent, LLP 1000; for the
history both inherit, LLP 0329 and its family.

## Non-claims

- This document accepts nothing in LLP 2000.000 on the author's behalf; it
  is the brief, and only running code binds.
- It changes no Snapback 2 or Snapback 3 status, scope, ceiling or gate, and
  edits neither rules file.
- It decides no promotion; the comparison and its ruling are the author's.
- It claims no result; every number above is a threshold or a plan.

    /// fired for is committed with the job row, so a restart never fires
    /// twice for one period; a schedule seen for the first time fires from
    /// its next boundary. Returns the names fired.
    pub fn fire_schedules(&mut self, now: i64) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        self.fire_schedule_range(now, 0..self.schema.schedules.len(), None)
    }

    /// One atomic schedule boundary, so the host can release its writer between
    /// schedules. A changed/shortened schema is revisited on the next tick.
    pub fn fire_schedule_at(&mut self, now: i64, index: usize) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        self.fire_schedule_range(now, index..index.saturating_add(1), None)
    }

    /// Admit one historical period on the current clock. In particular an
    /// action's deadline begins at admission, not at the historical boundary.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12.1 — period identity and admission time are distinct during explicit catch-up.
    pub fn fire_schedule_boundary_at(&mut self, now: i64, index: usize, boundary: i64) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        let schedule = self.schema.schedules.get(index).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("unknown schedule index".into()))?;
        if schedule.boundary(boundary) != boundary || boundary > schedule.boundary(now) {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input("schedule window must end at an elapsed boundary".into()));
        }
        self.fire_schedule_range(now, index..index.saturating_add(1), Some(boundary))
    }

    fn fire_schedule_range(&mut self, now: i64, range: std::ops::Range<usize>, selected: Option<i64>) -> Result<Vec<String>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut fired = Vec::new();
        for schedule in self.schema.schedules.get(range).unwrap_or(&[]).to_vec() {
            let boundary = selected.unwrap_or_else(|| schedule.boundary(now));
            let key = format!("schedule:{}", schedule.name);
            let shape_key = format!("schedule-shape:{}", schedule.name);
            let shape = digest(&schedule);
            let program = self.programs.get(&schedule.target).filter(|p| matches!(p.kind, ProgramKind::Job | ProgramKind::Action))
                .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("schedule {} names missing job or action {}; restore its target before firing it", schedule.name, schedule.target)))?.clone();
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action && !schedule.service.as_deref().is_some_and(crate::identity::is_service) {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a scheduled action requires its declared service seat".into()));
            }
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job && schedule.service.is_some() {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a scheduled job runs as the system, not a service seat".into()));
            }
            let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
            let previous_shape: Option<String> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1", [&shape_key], |row| row.get(0)).optional()?;
            if previous_shape.as_ref().is_some_and(|previous| previous != &shape) {
                tx.set_meta(&key, &boundary.to_string())?;
                tx.set_meta(&shape_key, &shape)?;
                tx.commit()?;
                continue;
            }
            tx.set_meta(&shape_key, &shape)?;
            let last: Option<i64> = tx.conn().query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = ?1", [&key], |row| row.get::<_, String>(0)).optional()?.and_then(|value| value.parse().ok());
            if last.is_some_and(|last| last >= boundary) {
                tx.commit()?;
                continue;
            }
            let Some(_) = last else {
                tx.set_meta(&key, &boundary.to_string())?;
                tx.commit()?;
                continue;
            };
            let mut args = BTreeMap::new();
            if program.args.contains_key("periodStart") {
                args.insert("periodStart".to_owned(), Value::Int(schedule.previous(boundary)));
            }
            if program.args.contains_key("periodEnd") {
                args.insert("periodEnd".to_owned(), Value::Int(boundary));
            }
            let id = crate::ids::mint(now, &mut self.minter)?;
            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Action {
                let principal = schedule.service.as_deref().expect("checked service seat");
                validate_inputs(&self.schema, &program, &mut args)?;
                super::actions::authorize(&program, principal)?;
                let raw = serde_json::Value::Object(crate::wire::map_to_json(&args));
                let context = Context::new(principal, args).at(now);
                // @ref LLP 3000.009#5.2 — the boundary and service-seat admission are one commit.
                super::actions::insert_request(&tx, &self.schema, &program, &id, &context, &raw, true, now)?;
            } else {
                #[cfg(feature = "native")]
                let program_hash = job_admission_hash(&program, self.native.as_deref().map(super::native::Module::digest));
                #[cfg(not(feature = "native"))]
                let program_hash = job_admission_hash(&program, None);
                insert_job(tx.conn(), &id, &schedule.target, &args, &program_hash, now)?;
            }
            tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _meta(key, value) VALUES (?1, ?2) ON CONFLICT(key) DO UPDATE SET value = excluded.value", params![key, boundary.to_string()])?;
            tx.commit()?;
            fired.push(schedule.name.clone());
        }
        Ok(fired)
    }
}

/// One chunk of a native job (LLP 3000.005): the module runs once with
/// the state it returned last time, inside one transaction under the job
//! Timing-critical maintenance and bulk catch-up share bounded engine slices.
use super::{Shared, lock, refusal_json, respond};
use serde_json::json;
use snapback4_core::RuntimeError;
use std::{sync::Arc, time::{Duration, Instant}};
use tiny_http::Request;

#[derive(Default, serde::Serialize)]
struct Work { delivery_chunks: usize, expired: u64, schedules: usize, chunks: usize, parked: usize, #[serde(skip)] fired: Vec<String> }

const WORK_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_millis(2);
const CONTENDED_TIME: Duration = Duration::from_micros(250);

// Charge consumed CPU for pacing, not time the OS already kept this thread off
// CPU. The cooperative chunk deadline still uses wall time. A failed clock
// sample falls back to the more conservative wall-time pause.
struct SliceClock { wall: Instant, cpu: Option<Duration> }
impl SliceClock {
    fn start() -> Self { Self { wall: Instant::now(), cpu: Self::cpu_time() } }
    fn cpu_time() -> Option<Duration> {
        let mut at = libc::timespec { tv_sec: 0, tv_nsec: 0 };
        if unsafe { libc::clock_gettime(libc::CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, &mut at) } != 0 { return None; }
        Some(Duration::new(at.tv_sec.try_into().ok()?, at.tv_nsec.try_into().ok()?))
    }
    fn consumed(&self) -> Duration {
        self.cpu.zip(Self::cpu_time()).and_then(|(start, end)| end.checked_sub(start)).unwrap_or_else(|| self.wall.elapsed())
    }
}

// Detached readers do not take engine tickets. Sample OS load as well as the
// round-3 engine contention signal; cache the syscall for 100 ms. Load includes
// other tenants and trails short bursts, so this is a pacing hint, not admission.
fn host_contended() -> bool {
    use std::sync::{Mutex, OnceLock};
    static SAMPLE: Mutex<(Option<Instant>, bool)> = Mutex::new((None, false));
    static CPUS: OnceLock<usize> = OnceLock::new();
    let mut sample = lock(&SAMPLE);
    if sample.0.is_none_or(|at| at.elapsed() >= Duration::from_millis(100)) {
        let mut load = 0.0;
        let cpus = *CPUS.get_or_init(|| std::thread::available_parallelism().map_or(1, |n| n.get()));
        sample.1 = unsafe { libc::getloadavg(&mut load, 1) } == 1 && load >= cpus as f64;
        sample.0 = Some(Instant::now());
    }
    sample.1
}

fn engine_work<T>(shared: &Shared, run: impl FnOnce(&mut snapback4_core::Engine, Duration) -> T) -> T {
    let mut engine = shared.engine.background();
    lock(&shared.readers).retry_missing(&engine);
    let budget = if engine.contended() || host_contended() { CONTENDED_TIME } else { WORK_TIME };
    let result = run(&mut engine, budget);
    // Publish from this guard: a second ordinary admission would both delay
    // notification and mistake maintenance for a newly arrived request.
    shared.publish_engine(&engine);
    result
}

fn phase<T: Default>(shared: &Shared, result: Result<T, RuntimeError>, drain: bool, name: &str) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
    match result {
        Err(error) if !drain => { super::adoption::background_failure(shared, name, &error); Ok(T::default()) }
        other => other,
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — lifetimes are swept into the partition log; missed schedule boundaries fire once.
fn work(shared: &Shared, drain: bool, tick: u64) -> Result<Work, RuntimeError> {
    let mut work = Work::default();
    shared.ephemeral.sweep(shared.now())?;
    work.delivery_chunks = delivery(shared, drain)?;
    loop {
        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_view_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "view recount")?.is_none() {break;}
        work.chunks += 1;
        if !drain && work.chunks >= 500 {break;}
        std::thread::yield_now();
    }
    loop {
        let swept = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.sweep_expired_for(shared.now(), 2_000, budget)), drain, "the expiry sweep")?;
        work.expired += swept;
        if !drain || swept == 0 { break; }
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — advancing a replay clock does not release its schedule hold.
    if !shared.hold_schedules {
        let fired = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedules(shared.now())), drain, "schedules")?;
        work.schedules = fired.len();
        for name in &fired { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: schedule {name} fired"); }
        work.fired = fired;
    }
    while drain || work.chunks < 500 {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
        let (run, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| {
            let run = engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget);
            (run, engine.take_native_logs())
        });
        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
        let Some(run) = phase(shared, run, drain, "job")? else { break; };
        work.chunks += 1;
        match run.state {
            snapback4_core::JobState::Done => snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} done: {} chunk{}, {} row{} written", run.name, run.chunks, if run.chunks == 1 { "" } else { "s" }, run.rows_written, if run.rows_written == 1 { "" } else { "s" }),
            snapback4_core::JobState::Parked => {
                work.parked += 1;
                if shared.personas { shared.refusals.job(&run); }
                snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: job {} parked after {} chunks: {}", run.name, run.chunks, run.error.as_deref().unwrap_or(""));
            }
            _ => {}
        }
        // FIFO engine admission hands waiting requests their turn before the
        // next chunk; yield_now alone cannot make that promise for a mutex.
    }
    if drain || tick % 5 == 0 {
        loop {
            let actions = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_actions_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the action lease sweep")?;
            let effects = phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.requeue_expired_effects_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "the effect lease sweep")?;
            if actions + effects > 0 { shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine)); }
            if effects > 0 { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: {effects} effect lease{} expired; queued again", if effects == 1 { "" } else { "s" }); }
            if !drain || actions + effects == 0 { break; }
        }
    }
    if drain || tick % 60 == 0 {
        phase(shared, (|| {
            let mut sweep = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.prepare_asset_sweep_for(shared.now(), budget))?;
            sweep.remove_files()?;
            while !sweep.is_empty() { engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.finish_asset_sweep_for(&mut sweep, budget))?; }
            Ok(())
        })(), drain, "the asset sweep")?;
    }
    Ok(work)
}

fn delivery(shared: &Shared, drain: bool) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
    let mut chunks = 0;
    while drain || chunks < 500 {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { break; }
        if phase(shared, engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| engine.run_timeline_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget)), drain, "timeline delivery")?.is_none() { break; }
        chunks += 1;
    }
    Ok(chunks)
}

// @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — ordinary-priority bulk work yields
// outside every shared lock. Aged service protects finite backlogs from traffic.
const BULK_AGE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
enum BulkKind { Delivery, Recount, Job }
pub(super) struct State {
    next: BulkKind,
    serviced: Instant,
    resume_at: Instant,
    empty_pause: Duration,
    advancing: bool,
    // Separate clock callers (and a dev reset) may wait here; maintenance never takes this lock.
    pub(super) advance: Arc<std::sync::Mutex<()>>,
}
impl Default for State {
    fn default() -> Self {
        let now = Instant::now();

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:12:Generations climb on every actual switch, including rollback and forward; selecting identical execution content is a no-op. `token --service ops --release ./deployment` works while serve runs. Signups are rate-limited: create proof accounts sequentially and respect a returned retry delay. Time-gated mutations on the real clock need a bounded retry of the authored refusal (for example `NOT_OPEN`), with a deadline and short delay; never retry unrelated refusals. Development proofs can advance the clock (`guide time`).
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:110:Previews lead with query/count active-table losses, linked dormant rows, then grouped safety dependencies. Verify filtered/paged/per-viewer results and denominators. Literal-default backfills print `table.column ← value on N rows` in previews and cold/hot deploy receipts: interpreted historical fields, not physical writes. Preview shows old and new rule clauses and mutation `require`s side by side, preserving Boolean parentheses. A proved tightening is a note, including `require A else X` becoming `require A else X; require B else Y` when A's refusal contract is unchanged; changed codes/messages still warn. A loosening or unproved implication is a WARNING, including rollback that removes a new guard. Unchanged clauses and consulted keys, fields, rules, auth and visibility are silent. Unread column domains and forward domain widening do not unprove a probe; narrowing still gets exact dormant-value/population warnings. Changed dependencies group once: verify refusal cases. Comparison never changes admission. Missing operations/schedules, populated removed tables, changed clauses and job bodies are named separately. An unchanged job compiling differently after a dependency change gets a note. Original read-through captures restore only with the same capture definition; new uncaptured rows still need safe audience correspondence.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/deploy.md:120:A running serve accepts `POST /jobs/<job>` with a full service token; the job executes as the system on its FIFO queue and server clock. Every full service seat can trigger system jobs, so provision these tokens only to trusted operators. Guest/account sessions, read-scoped tokens and personas cannot use this door. `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` remains development-only.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/search_all.md:5:`null`; hits, ordering, completeness, cut, continuation and freshness stay the bare page's. Current hits use ordinary server/device reads; departed hits use this device's held images. An expired or uncertain decoration becomes `null` immediately, including while a refresh is in flight or an offline answer is recovered. Because the deadline covers the page, the client conservatively nulls all point-dependent leaves, preserving nested objects and every literal or source field that does not depend on a point read. A masked decoration is a placeholder, not proof of absence. Page freshness does not prove that every decoration is current. A refresh restores values once a later deadline is proven. Both client tiers return the page from one-shots and keep it in watches, with the same freshness. Online watches retry values with capped backoff until confirmed, even if the client clock estimate is ahead. Decoration expiry never causes `E_QUERY_RETRY`; only source-page expiry can withdraw the page. The page remains one server call per publication attempt. The bare form is also valid. `check --cost` names the lifted plan and distinct-decoration reads. Table rules protect server reads; retained images confer no server authority.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rules.md:23:Beyond three hops, private/maintained fields and ephemeral targets refuse E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP with concrete mutation `require` checks. A rule says `=`/`!=` over `.col`, `viewer` and literals, `exists`/`created` probes and `and`/`or`/`not`; it orders (`<`, `>=`) only an insert row's own required int/time/decimal/money columns (`.stars >= 1`; an integer literal beside money is minor units) and `update (old, next)` images of the same types. The clock, arithmetic, helper calls and `else CODE` are E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR: a `require … else CODE` in the mutation (`snapback4 why E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR` prints the table of rule forms).
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rules.md:72:clock and the last atomically applied server time.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/presence.rs:31:For a proven necessary absence guard in those effects-only mutations, a held poll is unconfirmed: the push is recorded and deferred five seconds from the commit, and only a new poll arriving within that window cancels it. Grace alone keeps its deadline, up to 35 seconds. Cancellation records the `satisfied` outcome `presence_reconnected` (guide effects). The guard decision does not change the value of `connected[p]` in a payload. Only a proven necessary absence guard authorizes cancellation; payload-only reads and alternative guards retain their ordinary decisions and remain immediate. Grace deadlines and recorded reconnects survive restart; `clock advance` makes a waiting effect due along with its grace.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/presence.rs:36:In tests: `snapback4 clock advance 36s` or `POST /dev/clock/advance` with `{ "duration": "36s" }` closes the up-to-35-second deferral after a poll ends; open polls stay connected, and online client queries read the server's updated answer (guide clock). `snapback4 clock advance 6s` expires a held-poll effect deadline.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/witness.md:128:`status` reports the current publication as pending, passed, skipped, failed or unverified. It and `doctor` compare the published generation and Hermes hash with the latest proof; a missing or stale handoff reports no proof for the current export, including equal-byte re-exports and failed skip markers. `status`/`doctor` observe a consistent generation under a nonblocking shared publication lock; a commit in progress reports pending. `--wait` follows that transaction through verification and handoff, retrying changed observations for up to five seconds; a stable missing handoff stays unverified. `--wait` follows the current publication and returns 0 only for its passed proof (1 failure, 2 pending/skip/no proof). Older superseded results remain in their own run directories. A failed proof includes its log path. Doctor presents native status as information; use `status --wait` for the native-proof exit code. Background QoS lowers native work's scheduling priority; shared simulator services and I/O mean it is not a zero-contention guarantee. After maintenance quiesces, five full plus twenty compressed runs remain. Current-publication evidence stays full regardless of start order; active exports and pending publication transactions also retain their evidence. Retention runs inside native owner-death process custody with a 30-second work deadline followed by the existing bounded group drain. Every committed automatic export schedules maintenance, including a failed status handoff, after publication releases its lock and without waiting for maintenance. Requests coalesce into one pending slot beyond an active custodian. Busy requests record pending and return; one background observer probes for availability without starting a native work group. Unfinished or leased discoveries remain pending until covered; the observer waits for publication/run/export leases and live owners at a 100 ms probe interval instead of repeatedly starting workers. Every retention wait is on a live lease holder or birth-matched owner, and is bounded. The passive observer and its follow-up chain have one 60-minute deadline, including the launch gate; expiry leaves a loud pending request for a later export. Ownerless publication transactions recover automatically under their leases. Run initialization is leased through export-lease acquisition and journalling, with a five-second refusal bound for a stuck initializer. Retention claims per-run custody under that lease and releases it before recovery or deletion. Ownerless unfinished runs are repaired by a bounded scan: current evidence stays full, while empty or partial abandoned shells are removed and do not consume keep slots. Conflicting identities or persistent maintenance errors park with a diagnostic rather than spinning. Pending records are fully written before atomic publication; a scan keeps a durable claim until successful completion, and acknowledgement cannot erase a newer request. Legacy partial claims are repaired idempotently. A finishing custodian remains the observer's backup until a live successor scan has claimed pending work; a busy queue lock is not a handoff acknowledgement. Every export first inspects the actual full/compressed evidence, publication pins and live owners, and re-arms excess, stranded or parked maintenance even if no request was ever written and its Expo export later fails. Loss of both peers, a crash before a peer exists, or a parked error is retried by the next export; this is not a host daemon. The pending observer and active custodian supervise each other during a scan: if either dies, the survivor resumes the claim after native custody drains, without another export. Three interrupted attempts park with a diagnostic; a fresh export may retry. Known work errors park rather than retrying themselves. Readiness probes an exclusive publication lease, so shared holders also wait passively. Failed Expo wrappers remove their own private attempts under their lease; stale journals are classified by owner liveness, and empty failed attempts never occupy history slots. A claimed scan gets the 30-second work budget; proof-bound maintenance still defers immediately on a busy lock. Queue records and maintenance.log live directly under artifacts/native-smoke/. Both pre-driver and post-driver retention failures defer maintenance without failing the proof. Orphan deletion runs after releasing the publication lock. The public `witness export-retain` alias uses this same supervised wrapper; its internal worker requires recorded native custody. Cancellation and sleep cancel retention; newer publication also cancels proof-bound retention. A stalled compressor cannot hold the proof slot indefinitely. `native:smoke` always builds and waits; a cache miss in automatic smoke builds in the background.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/effects.rs:263:A held poll is unconfirmed: its push waits five seconds from the commit for a new poll to arrive. With no live poll but an unexpired presence grace, an absence-conditioned push is queued for the grace deadline: up to 35 seconds. `doctor` shows the deferred count and next deadline; `GET /effects` exposes each deadline as `nextAt`. In tests expire the held-poll deadline with `snapback4 clock advance 6s`; close the grace window with `snapback4 clock advance 36s` or `POST /dev/clock/advance` with `{ "duration": "36s" }`; a real-time poll budget must exceed 35 seconds. Only a poll arriving after the commit within its window cancels it, even if that poll ends before claim or the server restarts. The effect is recorded as `satisfied` with `result: { outcome: 'presence_reconnected' }` and zero attempts; it is never handed to a worker. Without a reconnect it becomes claimable once, using the existing lease protocol. No live poll and no grace means immediate admission. A poll already open at claim does not satisfy the effect.

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/codes.rs:316:    ("E_QUARRY_IMMUTABLE", "an immutable patch provably changes a stored value or supplies a fresh clock/id; omit it from the patch and set it on insert. guide writes."),
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/codes.rs:322:    ("N_SYNC_WINDOW_SIZE", "a note, not an error — check previews a new or changed sync window against an existing table at least ten times larger, across all groups. last N bounds each device partition, not server history; history queries with cursors page from the server beyond it, offline only within acquired data. Bounded probes inspect at most 10,000 row keys per changed window; unchanged windows and larger probes stay quiet. guide sync."),
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/codes.rs:323:    ("N_SYNC_ORDER", "a note, not an error — a device-capable query pages a windowed table on another index (including a calendar index) or oldest first. Whole-domain aggregates and word indexes have separate coverage requirements and are outside this ordering advice. Its acquisition boundary proves only newest-first pages on the window's own index, within that boundary. The note names both orders and the source site. A paste-ready page uses last N on the window index, audience equalities in bracket keys, and the original predicates and cursor. It requires a cursor, a cap covering the whole group, or the same ordered columns already read newest first. A cursorless partial page in another order gets words because reordering could select different rows. Restart pagination when changing order; the full traversal keeps the same rows. Only scope-independent range endpoints can move into residual filters. Outer-row endpoints, generated membership lanes, explicit merges and projected views get the window order and index in words without a paste-ready replacement; one lane is not the whole read. Capped pages get last N from their bound scan. Otherwise the ordinary query needs the server; offline coverage is unknown. A held query stays on the device and reports incomplete coverage without server fallback. online only currently applies to tables and views, not query headers. guide sync."),
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/codes.rs:326:    ("E_SCHEDULE", "a schedule must name a job or an action taking only `periodStart: time` and `periodEnd: time`, or nothing. An action requires `as service:<seat>`; a job runs as the system and forbids as. guide actions, jobs."),

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/E_QUARRY_IMMUTABLE.md:3:E_QUARRY_IMMUTABLE: An immutable patch provably changes a stored value or supplies a fresh clock/id. Omit it from the patch; insert it when creating the row. The static refusal requires a lifetime-free ordinary table without read-through and proven readability: a public/allow read rule, a system job, or conflict-key pins that establish the private read rule. Without that proof, private targets receive N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH because hidden immutable fields may be ignored. A literal or constant expression differing from a pinned key or the exact row just inserted is refused. Distinct fresh id captures differ too. A fresh clock on an immutable time column cannot preserve its original timestamp on later conflicts. An upsert with a proven fresh unused unique identity is insert-only and stays quiet. Exact target keys, current exact snapshots and proven equal values from an unconditional insert stay quiet. Intervening writes or uncertain control flow prevent snapshot proof; uncertainty receives N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH instead of a refusal. Source writers elsewhere do not constrain historical, seeded or adopted values. External IR keeps the established privacy exception: an unreadable target ignores immutable patch fields without comparing hidden values. guide writes.
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/N_SYNC_EMPTY.md:4:notices for one sailing. There is no bounded acquisition boundary for that
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/why/N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH.md:3:N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH: On readable targets, an immutable patch succeeds only when its value equals the stored value. For example, `upsert responses[key] { teacher: viewer, body }` succeeds for the original teacher; a different teacher's conflict is refused at runtime. Keep it when that equality guard is intended. Omit the column from the patch if another value may update this row; branch on a readable point and insert initial immutable values only when absent. Update obeys the same law. A value equal to the target key or a current exact snapshot stays quiet. Spreads and captured values receive the same analysis. Private targets keep this note unless conflict-key pins prove readability; a proven change or fresh clock/id then refuses instead. A value derived from the immutable parent selected by a pinned immutable reference stays quiet as an intentional runtime equality guard; this does not infer a historical parent/child invariant. This is a correctness review note. Unknown values, stale snapshots and other source writers' literals do not establish a mismatch. An unreadable target keeps the hidden-target exception: immutable patch fields are ignored without comparing stored values. guide writes.

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    fn engine() -> Engine {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table alpha:\n  at: time\n  public\ntable beta:\n  at: time\n  public\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema,
            bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program), 100_000).unwrap()
    }
    fn seed(engine: &mut Engine, table: &str, commit_at: i64, row_at: i64) {
        engine.seed_at([(table.into(), snapback4_core::value::object([
            ("id", snapback4_core::Value::Id(snapback4_core::ids::mint(row_at, &mut snapback4_core::ids::Mint::default()).unwrap())),
            ("at", snapback4_core::Value::Int(row_at)),
        ]))], commit_at).unwrap();
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_bounds_one_large_job_error_repeated_across_many_schedules() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..500 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        assert_eq!(crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap().len(), 500);
        let fired = engine.fire_schedules(60_000).unwrap();
        let message = "🧪\n\\".repeat(30_000);
        engine.store().conn().execute("UPDATE _jobs SET error = ?1 WHERE id = (SELECT id FROM _jobs ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC LIMIT 1)", [&message]).unwrap();
        let report = schedule_report(&engine, &before, &fired, 60_000, false, false);
        assert_eq!(report.len(), 500);
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["errorTruncated"] == true));
        assert!(report.iter().all(|s| s["run"]["error"].as_str().unwrap().len() <= REPORT_ERROR_BYTES + 3));
        assert!(serde_json::to_vec(&report).unwrap().len() < 2 << 20);
    }
    #[test]
    fn clock_report_refuses_before_the_host_encoder_limit() {
        let mut source = "table rows:\n  n: int\n  public 'fixture'\njob tick():\n  for row in rows:\n    update rows[row.id] {n:1}\n".to_owned();
        for n in 0..3000 { source.push_str(&format!("schedule s{n} every 1m: tick\n")); }
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let mut engine = Engine::new_at(snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), bound.schema, bound.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program), 0).unwrap();
        engine.fire_schedules(0).unwrap();
        let before = saved_boundaries(&engine);
        let refusal = crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, 60_000, false, true).unwrap_err();
        assert_eq!(refusal.code, "E_CLOCK_INPUT");
        assert!(refusal.message.contains("byte budget"));
        assert_eq!(before, saved_boundaries(&engine));
        assert!(REPORT_BYTE_BUDGET < crate::serve::JSON_RESPONSE_CEILING);
    }
    #[test]
    fn utc_text_and_spans_match_the_calendar() {
        assert_eq!(utc(0), "1970-01-01T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(951_782_400_000), "2000-02-29T00:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(-1), "1969-12-31T23:59:59Z");
        assert_eq!(utc(1_791_468_000_000), "2026-10-08T14:00:00Z");
        assert_eq!(span(12_633_000), "3h30m33s");
        assert_eq!(span(86_400_001), "1d1s");
        assert_eq!(span(0), "0s");
    }
    #[test]
    fn resume_offset_follows_the_newest_commit_and_is_never_negative() {
        let mut engine = engine();
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 0);
        seed(&mut engine, "alpha", 3_700_500, 1);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 100_000).unwrap(), 3_601_000);
        assert_eq!(resume_offset(engine.store(), 9_000_000).unwrap(), 0);
    }
    #[test]
    fn commit_tolerance_and_largest_lead_ignore_future_application_values() {
        let f=Fixture::new(&format!("discover-multiple-{persisted}"));
        let a=if persisted {f.start(&["dev","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"])} else {f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"])};
        let b=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
        let before_a=a.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap();
        let before_b=b.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap();
        let out=f.command(&["clock","advance","2h"]);
        assert!(!out.status.success());
        let error=String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
        assert!(error.contains("multiple live dev hosts") && error.contains(&a.address) && error.contains(&b.address),"{error}");
        assert!((a.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap()-before_a).abs()<100);
        assert!((b.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap()-before_b).abs()<100);
        output_json(f.command(&["clock","advance","1s","--port",b.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap()]));
    }
}

#[test]
fn clock_ignores_live_registration_with_wrong_project_or_reused_instance() {
    use std::fs::OpenOptions;
    let f=Fixture::new("discover-forged");
    let other=Fixture::new("discover-foreign");
    let foreign=other.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
    let local=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
    let dir=f.0.join(".snapback4/dev-hosts");
    for entry in fs::read_dir(&dir).unwrap() {fs::remove_file(entry.unwrap().path()).unwrap();}
    for project_matches in [false,true] {
        let instance=if project_matches {"wronginstance"} else {"wrongproject"};
        let path=dir.join(format!("{instance}.json"));
        let target=if project_matches {&local} else {&foreign};
        let mut record=target.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null);
        let record=json!({"instance":instance,"initialStoreId":record["initialStoreId"].take(),"project":if project_matches {json!(f.0.canonicalize().unwrap())}else{record["project"].take()},"address":target.address,"temporary":true,"pid":record["pid"].take()});
        fs::write(&path,record.to_string()).unwrap();
        let held=OpenOptions::new().read(true).write(true).open(&path).unwrap();held.lock().unwrap();
        let out=f.command(&["clock","advance","2h"]);
        assert!(!out.status.success());
        assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr).contains("no running dev discovered"));
        assert!(target.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap().abs()<100);
    }
}

#[test]
fn stale_instance_control_is_refused_before_clock_or_reset_changes() {
    let f=Fixture::new("discover-instance-race");
    let host=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
    let before=host.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null);
    for (path,body) in [("/dev/clock/advance",json!({"duration":"2h"})),("/dev/reset",json!({}))] {
        let reply=host.auth_request("POST",path,body,"x-snapback-dev-instance: stale-instance");
        assert_eq!(reply["denied"]["code"],"E_INPUT","{reply}");
        let after=host.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null);
        assert_eq!(after["storeId"],before["storeId"]);
        assert!(after["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap().abs()<100);
    }
}

#[test]
fn wildcard_memory_hosts_are_discovered_and_participate_in_ambiguity_and_cleanup() {
    for bind in ["0.0.0.0","::"] {
        let f=Fixture::new(&format!("discover-wildcard-{bind}"));
        let wildcard=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--bind",bind,"--i-understand-cleartext","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
        let dir=f.0.join(".snapback4/dev-hosts");
        let path=fs::read_dir(&dir).unwrap().next().unwrap().unwrap().path();
        let record:Value=serde_json::from_slice(&fs::read(&path).unwrap()).unwrap();
        let address:std::net::SocketAddr=record["address"].as_str().unwrap().parse().unwrap();
        assert!(address.ip().is_loopback());assert_eq!(address.is_ipv4(),bind=="0.0.0.0");
        output_json(f.command(&["clock","advance","1s"]));
        let persisted=f.start(&["dev","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
        let out=f.command(&["reset"]);assert!(!out.status.success());
        let error=String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
        assert!(error.contains("multiple live dev hosts") && error.contains(&address.to_string()) && error.contains(&persisted.address),"{error}");
        drop(wildcard);assert!(path.exists());drop(persisted);
        let _next=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
        assert!(!path.exists());
        output_json(f.command(&["clock","advance","1s"]));
    }
}

#[test]
fn stale_legacy_foreign_port_cannot_veto_a_valid_memory_registration() {
    let f=Fixture::new("discover-stale-legacy");
    drop(f.start(&["dev","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]));
    let other=Fixture::new("discover-stale-legacy-other");
    let foreign=other.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
    fs::write(f.0.join(".snapback4/serve.lock"),format!("{}\nhttp://{}\n",foreign.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap(),foreign.address)).unwrap();
    let local=f.start(&["dev","--memory","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
    output_json(f.command(&["clock","advance","2h"]));
    assert!(f.command(&["doctor","--latency"]).status.success());
    output_json(f.command(&["reset","--clock","reset"]));
    assert!(local.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap().abs()<100);
    assert!(foreign.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null)["clockOffsetMs"].as_i64().unwrap().abs()<100);
    let url=format!("http://{}",foreign.address);
    let explicit=f.command(&["doctor","--url",&url]);assert!(!explicit.status.success());
    assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&explicit.stderr).contains("serves project"));
}

#[test]
fn persisted_restart_nonce_refuses_old_latency_reset_even_with_the_same_store_id() {
    let f=Fixture::new("discover-latency-instance-race");
    let first=f.start(&["dev","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"]);
    let old=first.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null);
    let port=first.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap().to_owned();drop(first);
    let second=f.start_on_port(&["dev","--no-watch","--hold-schedules"],&port);
    let current=second.request("GET","/dev/status",Value::Null);
    assert_eq!(old["storeId"],current["storeId"]);assert_ne!(old["instance"],current["instance"]);
    second.query("time");
    let path=format!("/dev/latency/reset?store_id={}",old["storeId"].as_str().unwrap());
    let deadline=Instant::now()+Duration::from_secs(3);
    let before=loop {
        let snapshot=second.request("GET","/dev/latency",Value::Null);
        if snapshot["operations"]["/q/time"]["n"]==1 {break snapshot;}
        assert!(Instant::now()<deadline,"query latency did not settle: {snapshot}");
        std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(1));
    };

## Period boundaries and anchors

`periodEnd` is the scheduled firing boundary, not the invocation's finish time.
`periodStart` is the preceding boundary; the represented period is [periodStart, periodEnd).
Fixed `every N` schedules anchor to the Unix epoch in UTC, shifted by `at HH:MM`.
`every 7d` therefore fires on Thursday at 00:00 UTC (1970-01-01 was Thursday),
not seven days after deploy. `every 7d at 08:00` stays Thursday 08:00 UTC.
For another weekday, schedule daily and gate the job using day(periodEnd) % 7
(Thursday=0, Friday=1, Saturday=2, Sunday=3, Monday=4, Tuesday=5, Wednesday=6); never assume a deployment-time anchor.
Calendar month/year schedules use UTC month boundaries; `guide time` has their syntax.

## Operations

In tests, use `snapback4 clock advance <dur>` or `POST /dev/clock/advance`; each example line advances the running dev once.

```sh
snapback4 clock advance 36s
curl -X POST http://127.0.0.1:4400/dev/clock/advance -H 'Content-Type: application/json' -d '{"duration":"36s"}'
```

For development and tests, `snapback4 dev --clock-offset +25h` starts the
server 25 hours ahead of wall time. Mutations at `POST /m/<op>`
stamp `now` from this clock. The clock keeps ticking monotonically;
`snapback4 clock advance 2m [--port N]` moves a running dev forward and waits
for expiry sweeps and queued job chunks to finish. Missed periods fire once,
not once per skipped boundary; `--hold-schedules` still holds admission.
The response reports `now`, `advancedMs`, cumulative `offsetMs`, a restart reminder, work counts (including parked
jobs), and `schedules`: for each schedule, whether it fired, for which period
(`periodStart`/`periodEnd`; `skippedPeriods` counts earlier periods the advance
crossed, which do not run), its job run, or why it did not fire. Schedule times
are UTC: `every 1d at 06:00` fires at 06:00 UTC. A missed period you still need
runs with `snapback4 run <job> '{"periodStart":…,"periodEnd":…}'`. If maintenance fails, time remains advanced and the normal tick retries
pending work, so do not blindly repeat the advance. The offset belongs to
this process and resets on restart; supply it again when continuing a test,
or start `snapback4 dev --resume-clock`, which takes the offset from the store's
newest commit stamp (or saved schedule boundary). After a plain restart, a
schedule whose saved boundary is ahead of the clock is held: dev prints
`schedule <name> is held until …` at startup, `doctor` marks it, and the advance
reply names the wait.
Rows stamped under an offset keep their times after restart; use a fresh store per offset test.
Run time-travel suites on a disposable store and a separate port, from the project directory:
snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/jobs.rs
  rejecting the list would remove an already accepted and intended form.
- **2026-09-12 — jobs are sweeps, schedules are boundaries (lead, LLP
  1016 §4 carried).** A chunk is one page per commit with its cursor, as
  the system principal, over an index whose columns cannot move; a
  schedule fires once per period boundary of the server's clock, the
  boundary committed with the job row, so restarts never double-fire and
  a schedule first seen starts from its next boundary. No client calls a
  job.

- **2026-09-13 — one interpreter, hosted two ways (lead, after the author
  asked for a native Rust module for Expo and Exact2).** The core's
  interpreter runs over a `Rows` trait; with the `sqlite` feature off it
  compiles to wasm. Measured on the pinned toolchain: the device core as
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-windows");
    let source = "use personas alice\ntable windows:\n  start: time\n  end: time\n  at: time\n  public 'test'\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\ntable drivers:\n  n: int\n  cap 1\n  public 'test'\n  online only\nquery windows():\n  return windows first 100 by .id\njob record(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):\n  for d in drivers:\n    insert windows {start: periodStart, end: periodEnd, at: now}\nschedule minute every 1m: record\nschedule two every 2m: record\n";
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), source).unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let port = host.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap();
    host.post("/seed", json!([{"table":"drivers","row":{"id":"one","n":0}}]));
    let advance = output_json(f.command(&["clock", "advance", "3m", "--port", port]));
    assert!(advance["skewMs"].as_i64().unwrap() >= 180_000 - 100, "{advance}");
    let minute = advance["schedules"].as_array().unwrap().iter().find(|s| s["name"] == "minute").unwrap();
    assert_eq!(minute["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 1);
    assert_eq!(minute["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 2);
    for skipped in minute["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap() {
        assert_eq!(skipped["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap() - skipped["periodStart"].as_i64().unwrap(), 60_000);
        assert!(skipped["command"].as_str().unwrap().starts_with("snapback4 run record '{"));
    }
    let reset = host.post("/dev/reset", json!({"clock":"reset","seed":[{"table":"drivers","row":{"id":"one","n":0}}]}));
    assert!(reset["now"].is_i64() && reset["skewMs"].as_i64().unwrap().abs() < 1000, "{reset}");
    let advance = output_json(f.command(&["clock", "advance", "3m", "--each-boundary", "--port", port]));
    let mut expected = Vec::new();
    for schedule in advance["schedules"].as_array().unwrap() {
        assert_eq!(schedule["skippedBoundaries"], json!([]));
        for window in schedule["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap() { expected.push(window["periodEnd"].as_i64().unwrap()); }
    }
    expected.sort();
    assert!(expected.len() >= 4);
    let rows = host.query("windows");
    let rows = rows.as_array().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(rows.iter().map(|r| r["end"].as_i64().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), expected);
    assert!(rows.iter().all(|r| r["at"].as_i64().unwrap() >= advance["now"].as_i64().unwrap()));
    let status = host.request("GET", "/dev/clock/status", json!({}));
    assert!(status["now"].is_i64() && status["skewMs"].is_i64(), "{status}");
    let before = host.query("windows");
    let refused = f.command(&["clock", "advance", "10001m", "--each-boundary", "--port", port]);
    assert!(!refused.status.success());
    assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&refused.stderr).contains("10000 schedule windows"));
    assert_eq!(host.query("windows"), before, "preflight refuses before admitting any window");
}

#[test]
fn kit3_first_http_query_materializes_only_the_live_dependencies_it_encounters() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-opening");
    let source = "use personas alice\ntable announcements:\n  opens: time\n  until: time ?\n  note: text <=40\n  not before .opens\n  expire at .until\n  public 'test'\n  online only\nquery all():\n  return announcements first 100 by .id\nquery clock():\n  return now\n";
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), source).unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch", "--hold-schedules"]);
    let now = host.query("clock").as_i64().unwrap();
    let mut seed = Vec::new();
    for n in 0..20 { seed.push(json!({"table":"announcements","row":{"id":format!("row{n:02}"),"opens":now+1000,"until":null,"note":"revealed"}})); }
    seed.push(json!({"table":"announcements","row":{"id":"never","opens":now+1000,"until":now+500,"note":"must stay private"}}));
    let seeded = host.post("/seed", json!(seed)); assert!(seeded.get("denied").is_none(), "{seeded}");
    host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"2s"}));
    let reply = host.post("/q/all", json!({"args":{}}));
    assert_eq!(reply["complete"], true, "{reply}");
    assert_eq!(reply["data"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 20);
    assert!(reply.get("loading").is_none() && reply.get("pending_opening").is_none(), "{reply}");
    assert!(!reply.to_string().contains("must stay private"));
}

#[test]
fn kit3_each_boundary_action_deadlines_start_at_admission() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-actions");
    fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), "use server\nuse personas alice\naction ping(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time) deadline 1m <- viewer = 'service:clock'\nschedule pulse every 1m as service:clock: ping\n").unwrap();
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let advanced = host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"5m","eachBoundary":true}));
    assert_eq!(advanced["schedules"][0]["firedWindows"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 5, "{advanced}");
    let store = rusqlite::Connection::open(f.0.join(".snapback4/store.sqlite")).unwrap();
    let (count, failed, earliest): (i64, i64, i64) = store.query_row("SELECT COUNT(*), SUM(state = 'failed'), MIN(deadline) FROM _requests", [], |row| Ok((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?, row.get(2)?))).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(count, 5); assert_eq!(failed, 0);
    assert!(earliest >= advanced["now"].as_i64().unwrap() + 60_000);
}

fn kit3_large_clock_report_uses_complete_http_and_runnable_no_argument_commands() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-large-report");

    assert!(output.stdout.len() > 65_536, "fixture must exercise the former transport ceiling");
    let reply = output_json(output);
    assert_eq!(reply["advancedMs"], 120_000_000);
    let skipped = reply["schedules"][0]["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 999);

#[test]
fn kit3_large_clock_report_uses_complete_http_and_runnable_no_argument_commands() {
    let f = Fixture::new("kit3-large-report");
    let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
    let port = host.address.rsplit(':').next().unwrap();
    let output = f.command(&["clock", "advance", "2000m", "--port", port]);
    assert!(output.stdout.len() > 65_536, "fixture must exercise the former transport ceiling");
    let reply = output_json(output);
    assert_eq!(reply["advancedMs"], 120_000_000);
    let skipped = reply["schedules"][0]["skippedBoundaries"].as_array().unwrap();
    assert_eq!(skipped.len(), 999);
    assert_eq!(skipped[0]["command"], "snapback4 run tick '{}'");
    let replay = f.command(&["run", "tick", "{}"]);
    assert!(replay.status.success(), "{}", String::from_utf8_lossy(&replay.stderr));
}

#[test]
fn kit3_http_transitive_opening_dependencies_ignore_never_live_worlds() {
    let mut paired = Vec::new();
    for never_live in [false, true] {
        let f = Fixture::new(if never_live {"kit3-transitive-never"} else {"kit3-transitive-absent"});
        fs::write(f.0.join("snapback/schema.q"), "use personas alice\ntable grants:\n  ticket: int\n  opens: time\n  until: time ?\n  unique ticket\n  not before .opens\n  expire at .until\n  public 'scheduled grant'\n  online only\ntable tasks:\n  ticket: int\n  read <- exists grants[ticket = .ticket]\n  online only\nquery all():\n  return tasks first 100 by .id\nquery clock():\n  return now\n").unwrap();
        let host = f.start(&["dev", "--no-watch"]);
        let now = host.query("clock").as_i64().unwrap();
        let mut seed = Vec::new();
        for n in 0..20 {
            seed.push(json!({"table":"grants","row":{"id":format!("g{n:02}"),"ticket":n,"opens":now+1000,"until":null}}));
            seed.push(json!({"table":"tasks","row":{"id":format!("t{n:02}"),"ticket":n}}));
        }
        if never_live { seed.push(json!({"table":"grants","row":{"id":"secret-never","ticket":99,"opens":now+1000,"until":now+500}})); }
        let seeded = host.post("/seed", json!(seed)); assert!(seeded.get("denied").is_none(), "{seeded}");
        host.post("/dev/clock/advance", json!({"duration":"2s"}));
        let reply = host.post("/q/all", json!({"args":{}}));
        assert!(reply.get("loading").is_none() && reply.get("denied").is_none(), "{reply}");
        assert_eq!(reply["data"].as_array().unwrap().len(), 20);
        paired.push(json!({"data":reply["data"],"complete":reply["complete"],"meter":reply["meter"]}));
    }
    assert_eq!(paired[0], paired[1], "never-live occupants change neither answer nor query meter");
}

    let now = host.query("clock").as_i64().unwrap();

[workspace.package]
version = "0.4.31"
edition = "2024"
license = "MIT"

# The ordinary loop: incremental release builds so an edit re-links, not rebuilds.
[profile.release]
incremental = true

# Scale fixtures exercise the same debug assertions over production-sized
# populations. Optimize their code without disabling those assertions so the
# complete warm blocking suite keeps its 60-second contract.
[profile.test]
opt-level = 1

# Device scale fixtures spend most of their time in the device crate itself.
[profile.test.package."snapback4-device"]
opt-level = 3

# The device core as wasm: size first.
[profile.wasm]

opt-level = "z"
lto = "fat"
codegen-units = 1
panic = "abort"
incremental = false
strip = true

[profile.production]
inherits = "release"
incremental = false
lto = "fat"
codegen-units = 1
opt-level = 3
panic = "abort"
strip = true

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
scripts/test.sh                      # everything blocking (60 s budget); reports every failure, never fail-fast
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
# Linux qualification host: strace >=6.8 on PATH (audits every successful TCP listen).
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
